# Abundance after nonvanishing for compact Kähler fourfolds

Coordinate `EN-103-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-TO-M-0096` | `PM-034.14` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Abundance-after-nonvanishing-for-compact-Kahler-fourfolds-September-27-2026` | date 2026-09-27 | derived from the openai/math preprint Abundance-after-nonvanishing-for-compact-Kahler-fourfolds-September-27-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove semiampleness of the actual ℚ-Cartier adjoint $`K_X+\\Delta`$ of a normal connected compact Kähler klt fourfold whenever it is analytically nef and some positive Cartier multiple has a nonzero section. The boundary is effective and rational; the fourfold need not be projective or ℚ-factorial. In Iitaka dimension zero, a positive Cartier multiple is the trivial holomorphic line bundle.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove semiampleness of the actual ℚ-Cartier adjoint $`K_X+\Delta`$ of a normal connected compact Kähler klt fourfold whenever it is analytically nef and some positive Cartier multiple has a nonzero section. The boundary is effective and rational; the fourfold need not be projective or ℚ-factorial. In Iitaka dimension zero, a positive Cartier multiple is the trivial holomorphic line bundle.

- **claim-main-abundance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a normal connected compact Kähler complex space of dimension four. Let $\Delta$ be an effective rational Weil divisor such that $(X,\Delta)$ is Kawamata log terminal and the actual adjoint $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. If $D$ is analytically nef and $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$, then there is an integer $m>0$ for which $mD$ is Cartier and $$H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(mD))\otimes_\mathbb C\mathcal O_X\longrightarrow\mathcal O_X(mD)$$ is surjective at every point. If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then one may choose $m$ so that $\mathcal O_X(mD)\simeq\mathcal O_X$.
  - Explanation: For a klt adjoint on a compact Kähler fourfold that is analytically nef and has a nonzero plurisection, some multiple is globally generated, and in Iitaka dimension zero it is trivial. No projectivity or Q-factoriality is needed.
- **claim-supported-lifting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-supported-lifting)): Let $(V,B)$ be a normal irreducible compact Kähler dlt pair of positive dimension, with effective rational boundary and actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint $A=K_V+B$. Suppose that an effective nonzero rational $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $P$ satisfies $$P\sim_\mathbb QA,\qquad \operatorname{Supp}P=\operatorname{Supp}\left\lfloor B\right\rfloor.$$ If the actual restriction $A\|_S$ to the whole reduced subspace $S=\left\lfloor B\right\rfloor$ is semiample, then $\kappa(V,A)\geq1$.
  - Explanation: If an effective representative of the adjoint is supported exactly on the reduced floor and the restriction to the floor is semiample, the ambient adjoint has positive Iitaka dimension.
- **claim-floor-connectedness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-floor-connectedness)): Let $(T,B_T)$ be a normal irreducible effective lc analytic pair with actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint, and let $p:U\to T$ be a projective resolution with smooth source whose crepant subboundary $G$ has SNC support. Put $$R=G^{=1},\qquad H=\left\lceil -G^{<1}\right\rceil.$$ Then $H$ is effective and exceptional, has no component in common with $R$, and $$\label{eq:floor-pushforward} p_*\mathcal O_R=\mathcal O_{p(R),\mathrm{red}}.$$ In particular $R\to p(R)$ has connected fibers. If the pair is klt, then $T$ has rational singularities and is Cohen–Macaulay.
  - Explanation: The coefficient-one floor of a log resolution has connected fibers over its image, and klt spaces have rational singularities and are Cohen–Macaulay.
- **claim-threefold-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-threefold-contraction)): Let $(Z,\Gamma)$ be a normal connected compact Kähler klt threefold with effective rational boundary and actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint $A=K_Z+\Gamma$. Suppose $$\label{eq:threefold-exposed-input} \alpha=c_1(A)+[\omega]\quad\hbox{is nef},\qquad \omega\quad\hbox{is K\"ahler}.$$ Then there is a projective surjection $f:Z\to Y$ with $f_*\mathcal O_Z=\mathcal O_Y$, where $Y$ is normal compact Kähler with rational singularities, and a Kähler class $[\omega_Y]$ satisfying $\alpha=f^*[\omega_Y]$. The actual rational line $-A$ is $f$-ample. A compact curve is contracted exactly when its $\alpha$-degree is zero.
  - Explanation: A klt Kähler threefold with a nef class adjoint plus Kähler admits a projective contraction to a Kähler base with rational singularities, contracting exactly the curves of degree zero.
- **claim-finite-null-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-null)): Let $X$ be a normal connected ordinary $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler threefold with klt singularities. Let $\alpha$ be a nef and big real $(1,1)$-class. Suppose that $C=\operatorname{Null}(\alpha)$ is a finite union of curves. There is a proper bimeromorphic morphism $\phi:X\to X'$ to a normal compact analytic space such that $\phi$ is an isomorphism on $X\setminus C$, and the underlying sets of its nontrivial fibers are exactly the connected components of $C$.
  - Explanation: When the null locus of a nef and big class is a finite union of curves, it is contracted by a proper bimeromorphic morphism to a normal compact analytic space.
- **claim-no-null-surface** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-null-surface)): Let $X$ be a normal connected compact Kähler threefold, and let $\alpha$ be nef and big. If no irreducible surface has zero top intersection with $\alpha$, then $\operatorname{Null}(\alpha)$ is a finite union of curves, possibly empty.
  - Explanation: For a nef and big class on a Kähler threefold, absence of null surfaces forces the null locus to be a finite union of curves.
- **claim-conormal-layers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-conormal-layers)): Let $(X,S+B)$ be an ordinary plt pair, where $X$ is a normal connected compact Kähler space, $S$ is an irreducible effective $\mathbb Q$-Cartier prime, $B\geq0$ is a rational $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor not containing $S$, and $K_X+S+B$ is an actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint. Suppose $\dim S\leq3$. Write the actual residue adjunctions as $$(K_X+S)\|_S=K_S+\Theta,\qquad (K_X+S+B)\|_S=K_S+B_S .$$ Let $\phi:S\to W$ be a projective surjection to a normal compact analytic space with $\phi_*\mathcal O_S=\mathcal O_W$. Suppose that the actual rational lines $-(K_S+B_S)$ and $-S\|_S$ are $\phi$-ample. For $k\geq0$, put $$\mathscr I_k=\mathcal O_X(-kS),\qquad \mathscr E_k=\mathscr I_k/\mathscr I_{k+1}.$$ Then $S$ is normal, $(S,B_S)$ is klt, and each $\mathscr E_k$ is a rank-one reflexive sheaf on $S$. For any positive global Cartier index $q$ of $S$, there is a finite effective rational Weil divisor $\Xi_k$ on $S$, with $q\Xi_k$ integral, such that $$\label{eq:conormal-actual-identity} \mathscr E_k^{[q]} \simeq \bigl(\mathcal O_X(-kqS)\|_S\bigr)\otimes\mathcal O_S(-q\Xi_k), \qquad 0\leq\Xi_k\leq\Theta\leq B_S .$$ Here $\mathscr E_k^{[q]}=(\mathscr E_k^{\otimes q})^{**}$ is formed on $S$, and $\mathcal O_S(-q\Xi_k)$ is a divisorial reflexive sheaf, which need not be invertible. Moreover $$\label{eq:conormal-vanishing} R^i\phi_*\mathscr E_k=0\qquad(i>0,\ k\geq1).$$
  - Explanation: The conormal layers of a prime floor are rank-one reflexive sheaves described by an effective defect divisor bounded by the different, and their higher direct images vanish.
- **claim-analytic-thickenings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-analytic-thickenings)): For every $k\geq1$, put $$W_k=(\|W\|,\mathscr B_k).$$ Then $W_k$ is a complex space with canonical reduction $W$, and the canonical sheaf evaluation defines a proper surjective holomorphic map $\phi_k:S_k\to W_k$ with $$(\phi_k)_*\mathcal O_{S_k}=\mathcal O_{W_k}.$$ Its reduction is $\phi$. The quotients $\mathscr B_{k+1}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr B_k$ define closed analytic embeddings $W_k\hookrightarrow W_{k+1}$. They commute with the embeddings $S_k\hookrightarrow S_{k+1}$ and the maps $\phi_k$.
  - Explanation: The direct images of the floor thickenings are genuine complex spaces with compatible proper maps and closed embeddings.
- **claim-prime-floor-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-prime-floor-extension)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:conormal-layers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:conormal-layers">7</a>, there are a normal compact analytic space $Z$, a closed embedding $W\hookrightarrow Z$, and a proper bimeromorphic morphism $F:X\to Z$ such that $F\|_S$ is the given map $\phi$ followed by that embedding and $$\label{eq:extension-fibers} X\setminus S\simeq Z\setminus W,\qquad F^{-1}(\|W\|)=\|S\|,\qquad \|F^{-1}(w)\|=\|\phi^{-1}(w)\|\quad(w\in W).$$ The natural map $\mathcal O_Z\to F_*\mathcal O_X$ is an isomorphism, and the fibers are connected. For a sufficiently divisible global index $\ell$ of $S$, the actual line $\mathcal O_X(-\ell S)$ is $F$-ample; in particular $F$ is projective. More generally an actual rational line on $X$ whose restriction to $S$ is $\phi$-ample is $F$-ample. The target is in Fujiki’s class $\mathcal C$.
  - Explanation: A contraction of a prime floor extends to a projective bimeromorphic contraction of the ambient space onto a normal target in Fujiki's class.
- **claim-dominating-current-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dominating-current-descent)): Let $p:Y\to Z$ be a proper bimeromorphic morphism between normal compact complex spaces, with $Y$ Kähler. Let $\eta$ be a smooth real closed $(1,1)$-form with local smooth potentials on $Z$. Suppose that a positive closed current $T$ with local potentials represents the local-potential Bott–Chern class $p^*[\eta]$, and that $T\geq\kappa$ for a Kähler form $\kappa$ on $Y$. For every smooth positive Hermitian form $g$ on $Z$, understood in local ambient embeddings, there are a constant $c>0$ and a global quasi-plurisubharmonic function $u_Z$ such that $$\label{eq:dominating-current-descent} \eta+i\partial\bar\partial u_Z\geq c g.$$ This is a current in $[\eta]$ with local potentials, and it has the bigness property preceding (O. Das, Hacon, and Păun 2024, Theorem 2.29) [[DasHaconPaun2024](#ref-DasHaconPaun2024)].
  - Explanation: A positive current dominating a Kähler form descends along a bimeromorphic modification to a potential dominating any given Hermitian form.
- **claim-current-cone-slice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-na-slice)): Let $Z$ be a normal compact Kähler analytic variety with rational singularities, and let $\kappa$ be a Kähler class. In the finite-dimensional local-potential numerical spaces put $$C=\overline{\operatorname{NA}}(Z),\qquad \Sigma=\{z\in C:\kappa\cdot z=1\}.$$ Then $\Sigma$ is compact. If a real local-potential $(1,1)$-class $\ell$ is strictly positive on $\Sigma$, then $\ell$ is Kähler.
  - Explanation: A class strictly positive on the compact slice of the current cone is Kähler.
- **claim-supported-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-supported-model)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, suppose $\kappa(X,D)=0$ and the normalized rational divisor $M$ of a nonzero plurisection of $D$ is nonzero. Then there are a normal ordinary $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler dlt fourfold $(V,B)$, with effective rational boundary, and a nonzero effective rational $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $P$ such that $$A=K_V+B\text{ is analytically nef},\qquad A\sim_\mathbb QP,\qquad \operatorname{Supp}P=\operatorname{Supp}\left\lfloor B\right\rfloor,\qquad \kappa(V,A)=0.$$ The pair has the projective resolution described in Lemma <a href="#lem:special-resolution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:special-resolution">17</a>.
  - Explanation: In Iitaka dimension zero with a nonzero section divisor, a dlt model exists with analytically nef adjoint and an effective nonzero representative supported on the floor.
- **claim-positive-iitaka-semiample** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-positive-kappa)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, if $\kappa(X,D)\geq1$, then $D$ is semiample on $X$.
  - Explanation: Positive Iitaka dimension gives semiampleness via a dlt modification and a known Kähler nonvanishing result.
- **claim-supported-preparation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-supported-preparation)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a normal connected compact Kähler klt pair with effective rational boundary and actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint $D$. Assume $\kappa(X,D)=0$, and let $M\geq0$ be a rational $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor with $M\sim_\mathbb QD$. There are a projective modification $p:Y\to X$ with $Y$ smooth compact Kähler, an effective rational SNC boundary $B_Y$, and an effective rational divisor $P_Y$ such that $$K_Y+B_Y\sim_\mathbb QP_Y,\qquad \operatorname{Supp}P_Y=\operatorname{Supp}\left\lfloor B_Y\right\rfloor,\qquad \kappa(Y,K_Y+B_Y)=0.$$ The SNC support has globally smooth distinct components, and $B_Y$ has coefficient one on every $p$-exceptional prime and on every strict transform of a prime in $M$.
  - Explanation: A log-smooth projective modification carries a boundary and effective representative supported on its floor while keeping Iitaka dimension zero.
- **claim-outer-target-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-claim-outer-target-positivity)): Retain the current pair $(X_i,B_i)$, its effective rational divisor $P_i$ with the actual line identity in <a href="#eq:running-supported-representative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:running-supported-representative">[eq:running-supported-representative]</a>, the nef and big class $\alpha_i=c_1(A_i)+[\omega_i]$ with $\omega_i$ Kähler, and the selected ray and floor in <a href="#eq:floor-contraction-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:floor-contraction-class">[eq:floor-contraction-class]</a>–<a href="#eq:global-support-zero-ray" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-support-zero-ray">[eq:global-support-zero-ray]</a>. Let $c:X_i\to Z_i$ be the projective contraction with connected fibers just constructed. It is an isomorphism away from $T$, has the embedded floor image $W$ and the exact $\varphi$-fiber sets, and contracts precisely the curves in $R_i$. Its target is a normal compact analytic space in class $\mathcal C$ with rational singularities. For every other floor prime, retain the projective contraction with connected fibers supplied by Subsection <a href="#subsec:actual-floor-split" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:actual-floor-split">4.5</a>, whose target is normal compact Kähler and whose pulled-back Kähler class is the restricted $\alpha_i$. Then there is a Kähler form $\omega_{Z_i}$ on $Z_i$ such that $$\label{eq:outer-target-kahler} \alpha_i=c^*[\omega_{Z_i}] \quad\text{in }H^{1,1}_{BC}(X_i),$$ where the group is defined by local smooth potentials.
  - Explanation: The running class descends to a Kähler class on the contracted target, via nefness, a dominating current and positive top intersections.
- **claim-fiber-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fiber-support)): Let $h:W\to Z$ be a projective morphism between normal analytic spaces with connected fibers, let $F$ be a scheme fiber, and let $E\geq0$ be a rational $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $W$. Suppose that $E\cdot C\leq0$ for every irreducible curve $C$ in $F$. Then either $\operatorname{Supp}E\cap F=\varnothing$ or $F\subseteq\operatorname{Supp}E$.
  - Explanation: A divisor nonpositive on all curves of a connected projective fiber either misses the fiber or contains it.
- **claim-special-resolution** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-special-resolution)): The final pair $(V,B)$ has a projective log resolution $\pi:\widehat V\to V$ with the following properties:* 1. *the strict boundary and exceptional support have globally smooth distinct SNC components;* 2. *every $\pi$-exceptional crepant coefficient is strictly below one;* 3. *if $\widehat Z$ is an irreducible component of the nonempty intersection of distinct strict transforms of floor primes, then $\pi$ is generically an isomorphism on $\pi(\widehat Z)$.* *Moreover $\pi$ is an isomorphism over $U_V$.
  - Explanation: The final pair has a projective log resolution with globally simple support, exceptional coefficients below one and generic isomorphism on floor intersections.
- **claim-floor-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-floor-generation)): Let $(V,B)$ be a normal ordinary $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler dlt fourfold with effective rational boundary and analytically nef actual adjoint $A=K_V+B$. Suppose that it has a projective log resolution $\pi:\widehat V\to V$ for which the strict boundary and exceptional support have globally smooth distinct SNC components, every exceptional crepant coefficient is below one, and $\pi$ is generically an isomorphism on the image of every irreducible component of an intersection of distinct strict transforms of coefficient-one boundary primes. Then the actual restriction $A\|_S$ to the entire reduced floor $S=\left\lfloor B\right\rfloor$ is semiample.
  - Explanation: With the special resolution, the adjoint restricted to the whole reduced floor is semiample, with no ambient section needed.
- **claim-adjunction-slice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-adjunction-slice)): Let $Z$ be a normal Cohen–Macaulay irreducible analytic space of dimension $n\geq2$, let $D$ be a normal prime divisor, and let $B=D+B'$ be an effective rational boundary with actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint. Fix a divisible degree $m$, a local frame of $\mathcal O_Z(m(K_Z+B))$, and its meromorphic $m$-residue on $D$. For a prime $\Gamma\subset D$, a general point of $\Gamma$ admits a local slice by $n-2$ holomorphic parameters with the following properties. The slice $T$ is a normal surface germ, the cut $C=D\cap T$ is a smooth curve germ transverse to $\Gamma$, and the restricted line is the actual $m$-adjoint line of the effective sliced boundary on $T$. After division by the base parameter volume, the order along $\Gamma$ of the residue on $D$ equals the order at $C\cap\Gamma$ of the surface residue on $C$. For $n=2$, the slice is the surface germ itself.
  - Explanation: An adjunction coefficient can be computed on a normal surface slice with the residue order preserved.
- **claim-strata-adjunction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-strata-adjunction)): Every stratum $Z$ is normal and compact Kähler. For every allowed choice $\mathbf e$ there is an effective rational boundary $B_Z^{\mathbf e}$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \pi_Z^*(K_Z+B_Z^{\mathbf e}) &=K_{\widehat Z}+\widehat B_{\widehat Z}^{\mathbf e}, \label{eq:stratum-crepant}\\ \mathcal O_Z\bigl(m(K_Z+B_Z^{\mathbf e})\bigr) &\simeq\mathcal O_V\bigl(m(K_V+B^{\mathbf e})\bigr)\|_Z \label{eq:stratum-line} \end{aligned}$$ for all sufficiently divisible even $m$. Both identities are the actual meromorphic residue identifications. For the full weights write $B_Z=B_Z^{\mathbf 1}$ and $C_Z=\left\lfloor B_Z\right\rfloor$. The pair $(Z,B_Z)$ is dlt; its floor components are exactly the next incident strata. In a common degree, sections on those components that agree by residue on every subordinate stratum descend uniquely to a section on the entire reduced $C_Z$. The same assertion holds for the floor $S\subset V$.
  - Explanation: Every stratum is normal compact Kähler with a dlt boundary carrying the restricted line, and residue-compatible sections descend to the reduced floor.
- **claim-floor-torsion-free** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-floor-torsion-free)): Let $(T,G)$ be a normal irreducible compact Kähler lc pair with effective rational boundary, and put $C=\left\lfloor G\right\rfloor$ with its reduced structure. Assume that the pair is klt on $T\setminus C$. Let $f:T\to P$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers to a normal irreducible compact complex space. Suppose an actual positive adjoint multiple is pulled back from a line on $P$. Then $$R^1f_*\mathcal O_T(-C)\quad\text{is torsion-free on }P.$$ Here $\mathcal O_T(-C)$ is the ideal of the reduced floor.
  - Explanation: The first direct image of the ideal of the reduced floor is torsion-free on the base.
- **claim-horizontal-mori-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-horizontal-mori-model)): Assume that $C_0$ dominates $Y$. There is a finite sequence of projective divisorial contractions and flips from $Z_0$ to an ordinary $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler model $T$, followed by a projective Mori contraction $$u:T\longrightarrow W,\qquad g:W\longrightarrow Y,$$ where $W$ is normal compact Kähler and both maps have connected fibers. Write $G$ for the transform of $B_0$ and $C^+=\left\lfloor G\right\rfloor$. The pair $(T,G)$ is effective lc and klt off $C^+$; the divisor $C^+$ is relatively ample for $u$ and still dominates $Y$. All steps factor over $Y$ and extract no divisor. On a common projective resolution of the sequence, the full adjoints are equal as meromorphic pullbacks of the same actual line from $Y$.
  - Explanation: For a horizontal floor, a program over the semiample target yields a Mori contraction on which the floor is relatively ample.
- **claim-restriction-link** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-restriction-link)): For every stratum $Z$ of dimension one, two, or three, there is either no comparison or one proper bimeromorphic comparison between two normal components of $C_Z$, allowing a component to be compared with itself, with the following properties.* 1. *On a resolution of its graph with smooth source, the two pulled-back actual boundary adjoint lines are equal as invertible subsheaves of meromorphic pluricanonical forms.* 2. *For all sufficiently large divisible $k$, uniformly over sections, a section of $L_Z^k\|_{C_Z}$ extends to $Z$ if its two pullbacks agree under this comparison. With no comparison there is no matching condition.* 3. *When present, the comparison pairs two coefficient-one markings on general projective-line fibers of a projective Mori contraction on a bimeromorphic compact Kähler model of $Z$.
  - Explanation: Extension of a floor section to a stratum needs at most one birational residue comparison, coming from two markings on a Mori fiber.
- **claim-finite-isotropy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-isotropy)): For every $d\in\{0,1,2\}$, every $d$-stratum $Z$, and every fixed integer $k>0$, the image of the self-comparisons in $\mathcal G_d$ on $H^0(Z,L_Z^k)$ is a finite group.
  - Explanation: Self-comparisons act through a finite group on each fixed section space of the lower strata.
- **claim-class-link** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-class-link)): Let a link of a three-dimensional stratum compare non-Moishezon surface strata $Z_1,Z_2$, allowing $Z_1=Z_2$. Let $P_i$ be their smooth minimal models and $\tau:P_1\simeq P_2$ the induced isomorphism. For the classes in <a href="#eq:surface-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:surface-class">[eq:surface-class]</a>, $$\label{eq:class-mod-ns} c_{Z_1}-\tau^*c_{Z_2}\in\operatorname{NS}(P_1)_\mathbb R.$$ Here $\operatorname{NS}(P)_\mathbb R$ is the real span of the first Chern classes of holomorphic line bundles in $H^{1,1}(P,\mathbb R)$.
  - Explanation: Across a link the ambient Kähler classes agree on minimal surface models modulo the real Néron–Severi space.
- **claim-lattice-scalar** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lattice-scalar)): Let $P$ be a smooth connected nonprojective compact Kähler surface with a nowhere-vanishing holomorphic two-form $\eta$. Put $N=\operatorname{NS}(P)_\mathbb R$. Let $G\subset\operatorname{Aut}(P)$ be a subgroup. Assume either that $N$ is degenerate, or that there exists $c\in H^{1,1}(P,\mathbb R)$ with $c^2>0$ and $\sigma^*c-c\in N$ for every $\sigma\in G$. Then the image of $G$ on $\mathbb C\eta^{\otimes m}$ is finite for every $m>0$. More precisely, every volume scalar has order in the finite set of integers $n$ with $\varphi(n)\leq b_2(P)$.
  - Explanation: Under the class condition the automorphism scalars on pluriforms form a finite set of roots of unity.
- **claim-transport-lower-restrictions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transport-lower-restrictions)): Fix $k>0$ and $d\in\{1,2\}$. Let a tuple through dimension $d$ be compatible along all incidences, and suppose that its part through dimension $d-1$ is invariant. For any arrow of $\mathcal G_d$, transport of the tuple’s source component has the assigned lower restriction on every floor stratum of its target.
  - Explanation: Transport along surface comparisons preserves prescribed lower-stratum restrictions, even when a floor curve is contracted.
- **claim-generating-tuples** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-generating-tuples)): There is a degree $k>0$ and a compatible generating system of tuples through dimension three which is invariant in dimensions zero, one, and two.
  - Explanation: A compatible, invariant and generating system of tuples through the strata is built inductively using norm products.
- **claim-root-neighborhood** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-root-neighborhood)): Let $A$ be a compact analytic subspace of a Hausdorff complex analytic space $X$, and let $L$ be a holomorphic line on a neighborhood of $A$. Suppose a line $Q$ on $A$ and an isomorphism $\beta:Q^{\otimes r}\simeq L\|_A$ are given, with $r>0$. There are a neighborhood $U$ of $A$, a line $P$ on $U$, and isomorphisms $$\alpha:P^{\otimes r}\simeq L\|_U,\qquad \gamma:P\|_A\simeq Q, \qquad \alpha\|_A=\beta\circ\gamma^{\otimes r}.$$ If two root pairs are already defined near $A$, every prescribed power-compatible isomorphism between their restrictions to $A$ extends to an isomorphism on a neighborhood, uniquely as a germ about $A$. The root pair itself is not asserted unique.
  - Explanation: A root of a line given on a compact subspace extends to a neighborhood, with extensions of compatible isomorphisms unique as germs.
- **claim-all-orders** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-all-orders)): For every local root chart <a href="#eq:local-root" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-root">[eq:local-root]</a>, every integer $j$, and every $k\geq1$, the map $$\label{eq:all-order-surjection} g_*\mathcal T_{j,k+1}\longrightarrow g_*\mathcal T_{j,k}$$ is an epimorphism of sheaves of complex vector spaces on $U$. The neighborhoods used to lift a particular germ may depend on $j$ and $k$.
  - Explanation: Section germs lift through every finite order of the boundary thickening in every degree.
- **claim-finite-components** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-components)): Let $h:H\to T$ be a proper holomorphic map to a complex manifold, and fix $t_0\in T$. Suppose that near $h^{-1}(t_0)$ the reduced support $H$ has a locally finite family of closed smooth labels $D_\lambda$ which locally are its distinct SNC branches, and that every closed intersection of these labels is smooth. After restricting to a suitable open neighborhood $U$ of $t_0$, the support has finitely many globally smooth indexed irreducible components. Every closed intersection of the new components has finitely many connected components, all smooth and proper over $U$. A global relative Kähler form already given on the old strata restricts to such a form on the new ones. The open $U$ can be chosen inside any previously prescribed neighborhood of $t_0$.
  - Explanation: After shrinking the base, the support has finitely many globally smooth components with smooth proper closed intersections.
- **claim-residue-insertion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-residue-insertion)): For the data <a href="#eq:cover-layers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-layers">[eq:cover-layers]</a>–<a href="#eq:resolved-boundary" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolved-boundary">[eq:resolved-boundary]</a>, multiplication by $\tau$ gives a morphism $$\rho^*\mathcal O_Z(aE)\longrightarrow\omega_{\widehat Z}(H),$$ and residue gives $\rho_H^*\mathcal A_{-a}\to\omega_H$. The resulting map in the derived category of $E$ has a retraction in the $\tau$-character. Consequently, for every $j$, and in particular for $j=1$, it gives a split injection $$\label{eq:residue-injection} \tau_*:R^jg_*\mathcal A_{-a}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow R^jh_*\omega_H.$$ The construction is natural under the power-compatible root comparisons of Lemma <a href="#lem:root-neighborhood" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:root-neighborhood">29</a>. Under an ordinary product with a complex manifold $B_0$, it is the pullback construction for canonical sheaves relative to $B_0$. For absolute canonical sheaves the insertion and its retraction are tensored with $\omega_{B_0}$; in particular the product insertion has source $\mathcal A_{-a}\boxtimes\omega_{B_0}$ and target $R(\rho_H\times\mathrm{id})_*\omega_{H\times B_0}$.
  - Explanation: The obstruction sheaf embeds as a split summand of the first direct image of the dualizing sheaf of the resolved support.
- **claim-global-graph** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-global-graph)): Assume $b>0$, and fix any $t_*\in T=\mathbb P^b$. There is a coordinate-power map $\psi:T'=\mathbb P^b\to T$ of degree $r$ in each coordinate, with $R=\mathcal O_{T'}(1)$ and an actual identity $R^r\simeq\psi^*\mathcal O_T(1)$, which is unbranched over $t_*$, and the following data. On a neighborhood of the compact graph $\Xi=S\times_T T'\subset V\times T'$ there are the two full root covers and a projective resolution with smooth ambient source $\mathscr W$. The graph cut $H'$ inside the reduced inverse section divisor is compact and reduced SNC of pure dimension $\dim V-1$, with finitely many globally smooth components. The projection $p':\mathscr W\to T'$ is a submersion near $H'$, and every nonempty closed component intersection is smooth and proper over $T'$, carrying the restriction of one Kähler form on a neighborhood of $H'$.* *Locally near every compact graph slice over $w\in T'$, the resolved ambient data, including their projection and full tautological divisor ideal, are isomorphic to an open restriction of the ordinary product $\widehat Z\times U'$ for a local root chart. There the cut is $H^\flat=H\times_U U'$, and its actual dualizing line is $$\label{eq:graph-adjunction} \omega_{H^\flat}\simeq\operatorname{pr}_H^*\omega_H\otimes \operatorname{pr}_{U'}^*(\omega_{U'}\otimes\psi^*\omega_U^{-1}).$$
  - Explanation: A coordinate-power parameter cover unbranched at a chosen point carries one compact transverse graph with the needed product structure locally.
- **claim-filtered-snc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-filtered-snc)): For the preceding data, $(K,F)$ is a good filtered regular holonomic right module. Put $M^j=\mathcal H^j p_+K$ for any integer $j$, and give it the filtration induced by the filtered direct image. Every level cohomology map $$\mathcal H^j F_p p_+(K,F)\longrightarrow M^j$$ is injective, with image $F_pM^j$. The module $(M^j,F)$ has a finite filtration by $\mathcal D_T$-submodules, strict at every $F_p$, whose quotients are polarizable real pure Hodge modules. More precisely, the quotient with support-filtration index $\ell$ has weight $\ell+j$. There are canonical identities, with the first realized by that injection, $$\label{eq:lowest-direct-image} F_0M^j=R^jh_*\omega_H\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow M^j, \qquad F_pM^j=0\quad(p<0).$$ Here $R^j=0$ for $j<0$, and $\omega_H$ is the absolute dualizing sheaf.
  - Explanation: The filtered direct image of the support module has strict pure Hodge quotients and its lowest step is the dualizing direct image.
- **claim-real-pure-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-real-pure-vanishing)): Let $T$ be smooth projective, and let $(Q,F)$ be the right filtered module of one strict-support summand of a polarizable real pure quotient in Proposition <a href="#prop:filtered-snc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:filtered-snc">34</a>. Write $Z$ for its support and $w$ for its weight. It is isomorphic, as a right filtered module, to the prime holomorphic component of a pure object in $\mathrm{pHM}_Z(T,\mathbb R,w)$ of Sabbah–Schnell, attached to the same generic polarized real variation. In particular, for every ample line $N$ on $T$, every integer $p$, and every $v<0$, $$\label{eq:real-negative-vanishing} \mathbb H^v\bigl(T,N^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{gr}^F_p\operatorname{DR}_T Q\bigr)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Each pure strict-support summand identifies with a pure real Hodge module, giving negative-degree vanishing twisted by an ample inverse.
- **claim-symbol-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-symbol-kernel)): In Proposition <a href="#prop:filtered-snc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:filtered-snc">34</a>, assume in addition that $T$ is smooth projective, and fix $j$. Put $M=M^j$, and let $$\sigma:F_0M\otimes T_T\longrightarrow\operatorname{gr}^F_1M, \qquad m\otimes\xi\longmapsto[m\xi]$$ be principal-symbol multiplication for the right action. For every ample line $N$ on $T$, $$\label{eq:symbol-hom-vanishing} \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal O_T}(N,\ker\sigma)=0.$$ This assertion includes a coherent torsion subsheaf of the kernel.
  - Explanation: No ample line maps nontrivially into the kernel of first-order symbol multiplication on a smooth projective parameter space.
- **claim-obstruction-derivation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-obstruction-derivation)): Fix $k\geq1$, and assume <a href="#eq:all-order-surjection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:all-order-surjection">[eq:all-order-surjection]</a> for every smaller order, every integer degree, and every root chart. The connecting maps for the current order factor uniquely as maps of complex vector space sheaves $$\label{eq:obstruction-map} \delta_k:g_*\mathcal A_j\longrightarrow R^1g_*\mathcal A_{j+k} \quad(j\in\mathbb Z).$$ They form a degree-$k$ derivation from the Laurent graded algebra $\bigoplus_jg_*\mathcal A_j$ to its graded cohomology module $\bigoplus_jR^1g_*\mathcal A_j$. If $t_1,\ldots,t_b$ are local coordinates on $U$ and $F$ is holomorphic in those coordinates, then $$\label{eq:obstruction-chain-rule} \delta_k(F(t))=\sum_{i=1}^b F_{t_i}(t)\,\delta_k(t_i).$$ These maps and formulas commute with restriction and with the ambient power-compatible root comparisons. Vanishing of $\delta_k$ in all degrees proves the current order of <a href="#eq:all-order-surjection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:all-order-surjection">[eq:all-order-surjection]</a>.
  - Explanation: The obstruction to the next lifting order is a graded derivation obeying a chain rule in parameter coordinates.
- **claim-local-symbol** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-symbol)): Let $J=(\partial\psi_i/\partial w_j)$ and $J^\#=\operatorname{adj}(J)$. Under the preceding assumptions, the following identity holds in the right module $M^\flat$: $$\label{eq:adjugate-operator} \widetilde c\,\det J+ \sum_{i,j}(\widetilde e_i\,\partial_{w_j})J^\#_{ji}=0.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:local-symbol-zero} \sigma\left(\sum_{j,i}J^\#_{ji}\widetilde e_i\otimes\partial_{w_j}\right)=0 \quad\text{in }\operatorname{gr}^F_1M^\flat.$$ For $\psi=\mathrm{id}$, after the natural graph identification, the exact identity is $$\label{eq:identity-operator} c(x)+\sum_i e_i(x)\partial_{t_i}=0\quad\text{in }M^\flat.$$ When $b=0$, the same construction without graph equations gives $c(x)=0$ in its degree-one direct-image module.
  - Explanation: An adjugate identity forces the symbol of the weighted obstruction classes to vanish in the pulled-back module.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-conormal-layers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,S+B)$ be an ordinary plt pair, where $X$ is a normal connected compact Kähler space, $S$ is an irreducible effective $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier prime, $B\\geq0$ is a rational $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor not containing $S$, and $K_X+S+B$ is an actual $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint. Suppose $\\dim S\\leq3$. Write the actual residue adjunctions as $$(K_X+S)|_S=K_S+\\Theta,\\qquad\n (K_X+S+B)|_S=K_S+B_S .$$ Let $\\phi:S\\to W$ be a projective surjection to a normal compact analytic space with $\\phi_*\\mathcal O_S=\\mathcal O_W$. Suppose that the actual rational lines $-(K_S+B_S)$ and $-S|_S$ are $\\phi$-ample. For $k\\geq0$, put $$\\mathscr I_k=\\mathcal O_X(-kS),\\qquad\n \\mathscr E_k=\\mathscr I_k/\\mathscr I_{k+1}.$$ Then $S$ is normal, $(S,B_S)$ is klt, and each $\\mathscr E_k$ is a rank-one reflexive sheaf on $S$. For any positive global Cartier index $q$ of $S$, there is a finite effective rational Weil divisor $\\Xi_k$ on $S$, with $q\\Xi_k$ integral, such that $$\\label{eq:conormal-actual-identity}\n \\mathscr E_k^{[q]}\n \\simeq \\bigl(\\mathcal O_X(-kqS)|_S\\bigr)\\otimes\\mathcal O_S(-q\\Xi_k),\n \\qquad 0\\leq\\Xi_k\\leq\\Theta\\leq B_S .$$ Here $\\mathscr E_k^{[q]}=(\\mathscr E_k^{\\otimes q})^{**}$ is formed on $S$, and $\\mathcal O_S(-q\\Xi_k)$ is a divisorial reflexive sheaf, which need not be invertible. Moreover $$\\label{eq:conormal-vanishing}\n R^i\\phi_*\\mathscr E_k=0\\qquad(i>0,\\ k\\geq1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },

For any positive global Cartier index $q$ of $S$, there is a finite effective rational Weil divisor $\\Xi_k$ on $S$, with $q\\Xi_k$ integral, such that $$\\label{eq:conormal-actual-identity}\n \\mathscr E_k^{[q]}\n \\simeq \\bigl(\\mathcal O_X(-kqS)|_S\\bigr)\\otimes\\mathcal O_S(-q\\Xi_k),\n \\qquad 0\\leq\\Xi_k\\leq\\Theta\\leq B_S .$$ Here $\\mathscr E_k^{[q]}=(\\mathscr E_k^{\\otimes q})^{**}$ is formed on $S$, and $\\mathcal O_S(-q\\Xi_k)$ is a divisorial reflexive sheaf, which need not be invertible. Moreover $$\\label{eq:conormal-vanishing}\n R^i\\phi_*\\mathscr E_k=0\\qquad(i>0,\\ k\\geq1).$$",

      "exact_statement": "Let $(X,S+B)$ be an ordinary plt pair, where $X$ is a normal connected compact Kähler space, $S$ is an irreducible effective $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier prime, $B\\geq0$ is a rational $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor not containing $S$, and $K_X+S+B$ is an actual $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint.       "explanation": "The conormal layers of a prime floor are rank-one reflexive sheaves described by an effective defect divisor bounded by the different, and their higher direct images vanish.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-analytic-thickenings",
        "exact_quote": "For every $k\\geq1$, put $$W_k=(|W|,\\mathscr B_k).$$ Then $W_k$ is a complex space with canonical reduction $W$, and the canonical sheaf evaluation defines a proper surjective holomorphic map $\\phi_k:S_k\\to W_k$ with $$(\\phi_k)_*\\mathcal O_{S_k}=\\mathcal O_{W_k}.$$ Its reduction is $\\phi$. The quotients $\\mathscr B_{k+1}\\twoheadrightarrow\\mathscr B_k$ define closed analytic embeddings $W_k\\hookrightarrow W_{k+1}$. They commute with the embeddings $S_k\\hookrightarrow S_{k+1}$ and the maps $\\phi_k$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-floor-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:conormal-layers\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:conormal-layers\">7</a>, there are a normal compact analytic space $Z$, a closed embedding $W\\hookrightarrow Z$, and a proper bimeromorphic morphism $F:X\\to Z$ such that $F|_S$ is the given map $\\phi$ followed by that embedding and $$\\label{eq:extension-fibers}\n X\\setminus S\\simeq Z\\setminus W,\\qquad\n F^{-1}(|W|)=|S|,\\qquad\n |F^{-1}(w)|=|\\phi^{-1}(w)|\\quad(w\\in W).$$ The natural map $\\mathcal O_Z\\to F_*\\mathcal O_X$ is an isomorphism, and the fibers are connected. For a sufficiently divisible global index $\\ell$ of $S$, the actual line $\\mathcal O_X(-\\ell S)$ is $F$-ample; in particular $F$ is projective. More generally an actual rational line on $X$ whose restriction to $S$ is $\\phi$-ample is $F$-ample. The target is in Fujiki’s class $\\mathcal C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-floor-extension",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:conormal-layers\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:conormal-layers\">7</a>, there are a normal compact analytic space $Z$, a closed embedding $W\\hookrightarrow Z$, and a proper bimeromorphic morphism $F:X\\to Z$ such that $F|_S$ is the given map $\\phi$ followed by that embedding and $$\\label{eq:extension-fibers}\n X\\setminus S\\simeq Z\\setminus W,\\qquad\n F^{-1}(|W|)=|S|,\\qquad\n |F^{-1}(w)|=|\\phi^{-1}(w)|\\quad(w\\in W).$$ The natural map $\\mathcal O_Z\\to F_*\\mathcal O_X$ is an isomorphism, and the fibers are connected. For a sufficiently divisible global index $\\ell$ of $S$, the actual line $\\mathcal O_X(-\\ell S)$ is $F$-ample; in particular $F$ is projective. More generally an actual rational line on $X$ whose restriction to $S$ is $\\phi$-ample is $F$-ample. The target is in Fujiki’s class $\\mathcal C$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.14"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:conormal-layers\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:conormal-layers\">7</a>, there are a normal compact analytic space $Z$, a closed embedding $W\\hookrightarrow Z$, and a proper bimeromorphic morphism $F:X\\to Z$ such that $F|_S$ is the given map $\\phi$ followed by that embedding and $$\\label{eq:extension-fibers}\n X\\setminus S\\simeq Z\\setminus W,\\qquad\n F^{-1}(|W|)=|S|,\\qquad\n |F^{-1}(w)|=|\\phi^{-1}(w)|\\quad(w\\in W).$$ The natural map $\\mathcal O_Z\\to F_*\\mathcal O_X$ is an isomorphism, and the fibers are connected.       "explanation": "A contraction of a prime floor extends to a projective bimeromorphic contraction of the ambient space onto a normal target in Fujiki's class.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dominating-current-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p:Y\\to Z$ be a proper bimeromorphic morphism between normal compact complex spaces, with $Y$ Kähler. Let $\\eta$ be a smooth real closed $(1,1)$-form with local smooth potentials on $Z$. Suppose that a positive closed current $T$ with local potentials represents the local-potential Bott–Chern class $p^*[\\eta]$, and that $T\\geq\\kappa$ for a Kähler form $\\kappa$ on $Y$. For every smooth positive Hermitian form $g$ on $Z$, understood in local ambient embeddings, there are a constant $c>0$ and a global quasi-plurisubharmonic function $u_Z$ such that $$\\label{eq:dominating-current-descent}\n \\eta+i\\partial\\bar\\partial u_Z\\geq c g.$$ This is a current in $[\\eta]$ with local potentials, and it has the bigness property preceding (O. Das, Hacon, and Păun 2024, Theorem 2.29) [[DasHaconPaun2024](#ref-DasHaconPaun2024)].",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dominating-current-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p:Y\\to Z$ be a proper bimeromorphic morphism between normal compact complex spaces, with $Y$ Kähler. Suppose that a positive closed current $T$ with local potentials represents the local-potential Bott–Chern class $p^*[\\eta]$, and that $T\\geq\\kappa$ for a Kähler form $\\kappa$ on $Y$. For every smooth positive Hermitian form $g$ on $Z$, understood in local ambient embeddings, there are a constant $c>0$ and a global quasi-plurisubharmonic function $u_Z$ such that $$\\label{eq:dominating-current-descent}\n \\eta+i\\partial\\bar\\partial u_Z\\geq c g.$$ This is a current in $[\\eta]$ with local potentials, and it has the bigness property preceding (O. Das, Hacon, and Păun 2024, Theorem 2.29) [[DasHaconPaun2024](#ref-DasHaconPaun2024)].",
          "source_id": "PM-034.14"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $p:Y\\to Z$ be a proper bimeromorphic morphism between normal compact complex spaces, with $Y$ Kähler.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-na-slice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a normal compact Kähler analytic variety with rational singularities, and let $\\kappa$ be a Kähler class. In the finite-dimensional local-potential numerical spaces put $$C=\\overline{\\operatorname{NA}}(Z),\\qquad\n \\Sigma=\\{z\\in C:\\kappa\\cdot z=1\\}.$$ Then $\\Sigma$ is compact.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-supported-model",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, suppose $\\kappa(X,D)=0$ and the normalized rational divisor $M$ of a nonzero plurisection of $D$ is nonzero. Then there are a normal ordinary $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler dlt fourfold $(V,B)$, with effective rational boundary, and a nonzero effective rational $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $P$ such that $$A=K_V+B\\text{ is analytically nef},\\qquad\n A\\sim_\\mathbb QP,\\qquad \\operatorname{Supp}P=\\operatorname{Supp}\\left\\lfloor B\\right\\rfloor,\\qquad \\kappa(V,A)=0.$$ The pair has the projective resolution described in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:special-resolution\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:special-resolution\">17</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-supported-model",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, suppose $\\kappa(X,D)=0$ and the normalized rational divisor $M$ of a nonzero plurisection of $D$ is nonzero. Then there are a normal ordinary $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler dlt fourfold $(V,B)$, with effective rational boundary, and a nonzero effective rational $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $P$ such that $$A=K_V+B\\text{ is analytically nef},\\qquad\n A\\sim_\\mathbb QP,\\qquad \\operatorname{Supp}P=\\operatorname{Supp}\\left\\lfloor B\\right\\rfloor,\\qquad \\kappa(V,A)=0.$$ The pair has the projective resolution described in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:special-resolution\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:special-resolution\">17</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.14"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, suppose $\\kappa(X,D)=0$ and the normalized rational divisor $M$ of a nonzero plurisection of $D$ is nonzero.       "explanation": "In Iitaka dimension zero with a nonzero section divisor, a dlt model exists with analytically nef adjoint and an effective nonzero representative supported on the floor.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-supported-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be a normal connected compact Kähler klt pair with effective rational boundary and actual $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint $D$. Assume $\\kappa(X,D)=0$, and let $M\\geq0$ be a rational $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor with $M\\sim_\\mathbb QD$. There are a projective modification $p:Y\\to X$ with $Y$ smooth compact Kähler, an effective rational SNC boundary $B_Y$, and an effective rational divisor $P_Y$ such that $$K_Y+B_Y\\sim_\\mathbb QP_Y,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Supp}P_Y=\\operatorname{Supp}\\left\\lfloor B_Y\\right\\rfloor,\\qquad\n \\kappa(Y,K_Y+B_Y)=0.$$ The SNC support has globally smooth distinct components, and $B_Y$ has coefficient one on every $p$-exceptional prime and on every strict transform of a prime in $M$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Retain the current pair $(X_i,B_i)$, its effective rational divisor $P_i$ with the actual line identity in <a href=\"#eq:running-supported-representative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:running-supported-representative\">[eq:running-supported-representative]</a>, the nef and big class $\\alpha_i=c_1(A_i)+[\\omega_i]$ with $\\omega_i$ Kähler, and the selected ray and floor in <a href=\"#eq:floor-contraction-class\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:floor-contraction-class\">[eq:floor-contraction-class]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:global-support-zero-ray\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:global-support-zero-ray\">[eq:global-support-zero-ray]</a>.         "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to Z$ be a projective morphism between normal analytic spaces with connected fibers, let $F$ be a scheme fiber, and let $E\\geq0$ be a rational $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $W$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-special-resolution",
        "exact_quote": "The final pair $(V,B)$ has a projective log resolution $\\pi:\\widehat V\\to V$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *the strict boundary and exceptional support have globally smooth distinct SNC components;*\n\n2.  *every $\\pi$-exceptional crepant coefficient is strictly below one;*\n\n3.  *if $\\widehat Z$ is an irreducible component of the nonempty intersection of distinct strict transforms of floor primes, then $\\pi$ is generically an isomorphism on $\\pi(\\widehat Z)$.*\n\n*Moreover $\\pi$ is an isomorphism over $U_V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-034.14"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-floor-generation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(V,B)$ be a normal ordinary $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler dlt fourfold with effective rational boundary and analytically nef actual adjoint $A=K_V+B$. Suppose that it has a projective log resolution $\\pi:\\widehat V\\to V$ for which the strict boundary and exceptional support have globally smooth distinct SNC components, every exceptional crepant coefficient is below one, and $\\pi$ is generically an isomorphism on the image of every irreducible component of an intersection of distinct strict transforms of coefficient-one boundary primes. Then the actual restriction $A|_S$ to the entire reduced floor $S=\\left\\lfloor B\\right\\rfloor$ is semiample.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-adjunction-slice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a normal Cohen–Macaulay irreducible analytic space of dimension $n\\geq2$, let $D$ be a normal prime divisor, and let $B=D+B'$ be an effective rational boundary with actual $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint. Fix a divisible degree $m$, a local frame of $\\mathcal O_Z(m(K_Z+B))$, and its meromorphic $m$-residue on $D$. For a prime $\\Gamma\\subset D$, a general point of $\\Gamma$ admits a local slice by $n-2$ holomorphic parameters with the following properties. The slice $T$ is a normal surface germ, the cut $C=D\\cap T$ is a smooth curve germ transverse to $\\Gamma$, and the restricted line is the actual $m$-adjoint line of the effective sliced boundary on $T$. After division by the base parameter volume, the order along $\\Gamma$ of the residue on $D$ equals the order at $C\\cap\\Gamma$ of the surface residue on $C$. For $n=2$, the slice is the surface germ itself.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

After division by the base parameter volume, the order along $\\Gamma$ of the residue on $D$ equals the order at $C\\cap\\Gamma$ of the surface residue on $C$. For $n=2$, the slice is the surface germ itself.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-strata-adjunction",
        "exact_quote": "Every stratum $Z$ is normal and compact Kähler. For every allowed choice $\\mathbf e$ there is an effective rational boundary $B_Z^{\\mathbf e}$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\pi_Z^*(K_Z+B_Z^{\\mathbf e})\n   &=K_{\\widehat Z}+\\widehat B_{\\widehat Z}^{\\mathbf e},\n       \\label{eq:stratum-crepant}\\\\\n \\mathcal O_Z\\bigl(m(K_Z+B_Z^{\\mathbf e})\\bigr)\n   &\\simeq\\mathcal O_V\\bigl(m(K_V+B^{\\mathbf e})\\bigr)|_Z\n       \\label{eq:stratum-line}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for all sufficiently divisible even $m$. Both identities are the actual meromorphic residue identifications. For the full weights write $B_Z=B_Z^{\\mathbf 1}$ and $C_Z=\\left\\lfloor B_Z\\right\\rfloor$. The pair $(Z,B_Z)$ is dlt; its floor components are exactly the next incident strata. In a common degree, sections on those components that agree by residue on every subordinate stratum descend uniquely to a section on the entire reduced $C_Z$. The same assertion holds for the floor $S\\subset V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-floor-torsion-free",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,G)$ be a normal irreducible compact Kähler lc pair with effective rational boundary, and put $C=\\left\\lfloor G\\right\\rfloor$ with its reduced structure. Assume that the pair is klt on $T\\setminus C$. Let $f:T\\to P$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers to a normal irreducible compact complex space. Suppose an actual positive adjoint multiple is pulled back from a line on $P$. Then $$R^1f_*\\mathcal O_T(-C)\\quad\\text{is torsion-free on }P.$$ Here $\\mathcal O_T(-C)$ is the ideal of the reduced floor.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-horizontal-mori-model",
        "exact_quote": "Assume that $C_0$ dominates $Y$. There is a finite sequence of projective divisorial contractions and flips from $Z_0$ to an ordinary $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler model $T$, followed by a projective Mori contraction $$u:T\\longrightarrow W,\\qquad g:W\\longrightarrow Y,$$ where $W$ is normal compact Kähler and both maps have connected fibers. Write $G$ for the transform of $B_0$ and $C^+=\\left\\lfloor G\\right\\rfloor$. The pair $(T,G)$ is effective lc and klt off $C^+$; the divisor $C^+$ is relatively ample for $u$ and still dominates $Y$. All steps factor over $Y$ and extract no divisor. On a common projective resolution of the sequence, the full adjoints are equal as meromorphic pullbacks of the same actual line from $Y$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-horizontal-mori-model",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-restriction-link",
        "exact_quote": "For every stratum $Z$ of dimension one, two, or three, there is either no comparison or one proper bimeromorphic comparison between two normal components of $C_Z$, allowing a component to be compared with itself, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *On a resolution of its graph with smooth source, the two pulled-back actual boundary adjoint lines are equal as invertible subsheaves of meromorphic pluricanonical forms.*\n\n2.  *For all sufficiently large divisible $k$, uniformly over sections, a section of $L_Z^k|_{C_Z}$ extends to $Z$ if its two pullbacks agree under this comparison. With no comparison there is no matching condition.*\n\n3.  *When present, the comparison pairs two coefficient-one markings on general projective-line fibers of a projective Mori contraction on a bimeromorphic compact Kähler model of $Z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-restriction-link",
          "exact_quote": "For every stratum $Z$ of dimension one, two, or three, there is either no comparison or one proper bimeromorphic comparison between two normal components of $C_Z$, allowing a component to be compared with itself, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *On a resolution of its graph with smooth source, the two pulled-back actual boundary adjoint lines are equal as invertible subsheaves of meromorphic pluricanonical forms.*\n\n2.  *For all sufficiently large divisible $k$, uniformly over sections, a section of $L_Z^k|_{C_Z}$ extends to $Z$ if its two pullbacks agree under this comparison. With no comparison there is no matching condition.*\n\n3.  *When present, the comparison pairs two coefficient-one markings on general projective-line fibers of a projective Mori contraction on a bimeromorphic compact Kähler model of $Z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.14"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every stratum $Z$ of dimension one, two, or three, there is either no comparison or one proper bimeromorphic comparison between two normal components of $C_Z$, allowing a component to be compared with itself, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *On a resolution of its graph with smooth source, the two pulled-back actual boundary adjoint lines are equal as invertible subsheaves of meromorphic pluricanonical forms.*\n\n2.  *For all sufficiently large divisible $k$, uniformly over sections, a section of $L_Z^k|_{C_Z}$ extends to $Z$ if its two pullbacks agree under this comparison.         "exact_quote": "Let a link of a three-dimensional stratum compare non-Moishezon surface strata $Z_1,Z_2$, allowing $Z_1=Z_2$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lattice-scalar",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a smooth connected nonprojective compact Kähler surface with a nowhere-vanishing holomorphic two-form $\\eta$. Put $N=\\operatorname{NS}(P)_\\mathbb R$. Let $G\\subset\\operatorname{Aut}(P)$ be a subgroup. Assume either that $N$ is degenerate, or that there exists $c\\in H^{1,1}(P,\\mathbb R)$ with $c^2>0$ and $\\sigma^*c-c\\in N$ for every $\\sigma\\in G$. Then the image of $G$ on $\\mathbb C\\eta^{\\otimes m}$ is finite for every $m>0$. More precisely, every volume scalar has order in the finite set of integers $n$ with $\\varphi(n)\\leq b_2(P)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-root-neighborhood",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a compact analytic subspace of a Hausdorff complex analytic space $X$, and let $L$ be a holomorphic line on a neighborhood of $A$. There are a neighborhood $U$ of $A$, a line $P$ on $U$, and isomorphisms $$\\alpha:P^{\\otimes r}\\simeq L|_U,\\qquad \\gamma:P|_A\\simeq Q,\n \\qquad \\alpha|_A=\\beta\\circ\\gamma^{\\otimes r}.$$ If two root pairs are already defined near $A$, every prescribed power-compatible isomorphism between their restrictions to $A$ extends to an isomorphism on a neighborhood, uniquely as a germ about $A$. The root pair itself is not asserted unique.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"

The root pair itself is not asserted unique.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-components",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h:H\\to T$ be a proper holomorphic map to a complex manifold, and fix $t_0\\in T$. Suppose that near $h^{-1}(t_0)$ the reduced support $H$ has a locally finite family of closed smooth labels $D_\\lambda$ which locally are its distinct SNC branches, and that every closed intersection of these labels is smooth. After restricting to a suitable open neighborhood $U$ of $t_0$, the support has finitely many globally smooth indexed irreducible components. Every closed intersection of the new components has finitely many connected components, all smooth and proper over $U$. A global relative Kähler form already given on the old strata restricts to such a form on the new ones. The open $U$ can be chosen inside any previously prescribed neighborhood of $t_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-residue-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "For the data <a href=\"#eq:cover-layers\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cover-layers\">[eq:cover-layers]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:resolved-boundary\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:resolved-boundary\">[eq:resolved-boundary]</a>, multiplication by $\\tau$ gives a morphism $$\\rho^*\\mathcal O_Z(aE)\\longrightarrow\\omega_{\\widehat Z}(H),$$ and residue gives $\\rho_H^*\\mathcal A_{-a}\\to\\omega_H$. The resulting map in the derived category of $E$ has a retraction in the $\\tau$-character. Consequently, for every $j$, and in particular for $j=1$, it gives a split injection $$\\label{eq:residue-injection}\n \\tau_*:R^jg_*\\mathcal A_{-a}\\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow R^jh_*\\omega_H.$$ The construction is natural under the power-compatible root comparisons of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:root-neighborhood\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:root-neighborhood\">29</a>. Under an ordinary product with a complex manifold $B_0$, it is the pullback construction for canonical sheaves relative to $B_0$. For absolute canonical sheaves the insertion and its retraction are tensored with $\\omega_{B_0}$; in particular the product insertion has source $\\mathcal A_{-a}\\boxtimes\\omega_{B_0}$ and target $R(\\rho_H\\times\\mathrm{id})_*\\omega_{H\\times B_0}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-graph",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $b>0$, and fix any $t_*\\in T=\\mathbb P^b$. There is a coordinate-power map $\\psi:T'=\\mathbb P^b\\to T$ of degree $r$ in each coordinate, with $R=\\mathcal O_{T'}(1)$ and an actual identity $R^r\\simeq\\psi^*\\mathcal O_T(1)$, which is unbranched over $t_*$, and the following data. On a neighborhood of the compact graph $\\Xi=S\\times_T T'\\subset V\\times T'$ there are the two full root covers and a projective resolution with smooth ambient source $\\mathscr W$. The graph cut $H'$ inside the reduced inverse section divisor is compact and reduced SNC of pure dimension $\\dim V-1$, with finitely many globally smooth components. The projection $p':\\mathscr W\\to T'$ is a submersion near $H'$, and every nonempty closed component intersection is smooth and proper over $T'$, carrying the restriction of one Kähler form on a neighborhood of $H'$.*\n\n\n*Locally near every compact graph slice over $w\\in T'$, the resolved ambient data, including their projection and full tautological divisor ideal, are isomorphic to an open restriction of the ordinary product $\\widehat Z\\times U'$ for a local root chart. There the cut is $H^\\flat=H\\times_U U'$, and its actual dualizing line is $$\\label{eq:graph-adjunction}\n \\omega_{H^\\flat}\\simeq\\operatorname{pr}_H^*\\omega_H\\otimes\n \\operatorname{pr}_{U'}^*(\\omega_{U'}\\otimes\\psi^*\\omega_U^{-1}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The graph cut $H'$ inside the reduced inverse section divisor is compact and reduced SNC of pure dimension $\\dim V-1$, with finitely many globally smooth components. The projection $p':\\mathscr W\\to T'$ is a submersion near $H'$, and every nonempty closed component intersection is smooth and proper over $T'$, carrying the restriction of one Kähler form on a neighborhood of $H'$.*\n\n\n*Locally near every compact graph slice over $w\\in T'$, the resolved ambient data, including their projection and full tautological divisor ideal, are isomorphic to an open restriction of the ordinary product $\\widehat Z\\times U'$ for a local root chart.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-filtered-snc",
        "exact_quote": "For the preceding data, $(K,F)$ is a good filtered regular holonomic right module. Put $M^j=\\mathcal H^j p_+K$ for any integer $j$, and give it the filtration induced by the filtered direct image. Every level cohomology map $$\\mathcal H^j F_p p_+(K,F)\\longrightarrow M^j$$ is injective, with image $F_pM^j$. The module $(M^j,F)$ has a finite filtration by $\\mathcal D_T$-submodules, strict at every $F_p$, whose quotients are polarizable real pure Hodge modules. More precisely, the quotient with support-filtration index $\\ell$ has weight $\\ell+j$. There are canonical identities, with the first realized by that injection, $$\\label{eq:lowest-direct-image}\n F_0M^j=R^jh_*\\omega_H\\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow M^j,\n \\qquad F_pM^j=0\\quad(p<0).$$ Here $R^j=0$ for $j<0$, and $\\omega_H$ is the absolute dualizing sheaf.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-filtered-snc",
          "exact_quote": "For the preceding data, $(K,F)$ is a good filtered regular holonomic right module. Every level cohomology map $$\\mathcal H^j F_p p_+(K,F)\\longrightarrow M^j$$ is injective, with image $F_pM^j$. The module $(M^j,F)$ has a finite filtration by $\\mathcal D_T$-submodules, strict at every $F_p$, whose quotients are polarizable real pure Hodge modules. More precisely, the quotient with support-filtration index $\\ell$ has weight $\\ell+j$. There are canonical identities, with the first realized by that injection, $$\\label{eq:lowest-direct-image}\n F_0M^j=R^jh_*\\omega_H\\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow M^j,\n \\qquad F_pM^j=0\\quad(p<0).$$ Here $R^j=0$ for $j<0$, and $\\omega_H$ is the absolute dualizing sheaf.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.14"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-real-pure-vanishing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be smooth projective, and let $(Q,F)$ be the right filtered module of one strict-support summand of a polarizable real pure quotient in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:filtered-snc\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:filtered-snc\">34</a>. Write $Z$ for its support and $w$ for its weight. It is isomorphic, as a right filtered module, to the prime holomorphic component of a pure object in $\\mathrm{pHM}_Z(T,\\mathbb R,w)$ of Sabbah–Schnell, attached to the same generic polarized real variation. In particular, for every ample line $N$ on $T$, every integer $p$, and every $v<0$, $$\\label{eq:real-negative-vanishing}\n \\mathbb H^v\\bigl(T,N^{-1}\\otimes\\operatorname{gr}^F_p\\operatorname{DR}_T Q\\bigr)=0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-real-pure-vanishing",

Write $Z$ for its support and $w$ for its weight. It is isomorphic, as a right filtered module, to the prime holomorphic component of a pure object in $\\mathrm{pHM}_Z(T,\\mathbb R,w)$ of Sabbah–Schnell, attached to the same generic polarized real variation.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-symbol-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "In Proposition <a href=\"#prop:filtered-snc\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:filtered-snc\">34</a>, assume in addition that $T$ is smooth projective, and fix $j$. Put $M=M^j$, and let $$\\sigma:F_0M\\otimes T_T\\longrightarrow\\operatorname{gr}^F_1M,\n \\qquad m\\otimes\\xi\\longmapsto[m\\xi]$$ be principal-symbol multiplication for the right action. For every ample line $N$ on $T$, $$\\label{eq:symbol-hom-vanishing}\n \\operatorname{Hom}_{\\mathcal O_T}(N,\\ker\\sigma)=0.$$ This assertion includes a coherent torsion subsheaf of the kernel.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-obstruction-derivation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\geq1$, and assume <a href=\"#eq:all-order-surjection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:all-order-surjection\">[eq:all-order-surjection]</a> for every smaller order, every integer degree, and every root chart. The connecting maps for the current order factor uniquely as maps of complex vector space sheaves $$\\label{eq:obstruction-map}\n \\delta_k:g_*\\mathcal A_j\\longrightarrow R^1g_*\\mathcal A_{j+k}\n \\quad(j\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ They form a degree-$k$ derivation from the Laurent graded algebra $\\bigoplus_jg_*\\mathcal A_j$ to its graded cohomology module $\\bigoplus_jR^1g_*\\mathcal A_j$. If $t_1,\\ldots,t_b$ are local coordinates on $U$ and $F$ is holomorphic in those coordinates, then $$\\label{eq:obstruction-chain-rule}\n \\delta_k(F(t))=\\sum_{i=1}^b F_{t_i}(t)\\,\\delta_k(t_i).$$ These maps and formulas commute with restriction and with the ambient power-compatible root comparisons. Vanishing of $\\delta_k$ in all degrees proves the current order of <a href=\"#eq:all-order-surjection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:all-order-surjection\">[eq:all-order-surjection]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.14"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-obstruction-derivation",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\geq1$, and assume <a href=\"#eq:all-order-surjection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:all-order-surjection\">[eq:all-order-surjection]</a> for every smaller order, every integer degree, and every root chart. The connecting maps for the current order factor uniquely as maps of complex vector space sheaves $$\\label{eq:obstruction-map}\n \\delta_k:g_*\\mathcal A_j\\longrightarrow R^1g_*\\mathcal A_{j+k}\n \\quad(j\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ They form a degree-$k$ derivation from the Laurent graded algebra $\\bigoplus_jg_*\\mathcal A_j$ to its graded cohomology module $\\bigoplus_jR^1g_*\\mathcal A_j$. If $t_1,\\ldots,t_b$ are local coordinates on $U$ and $F$ is holomorphic in those coordinates, then $$\\label{eq:obstruction-chain-rule}\n \\delta_k(F(t))=\\sum_{i=1}^b F_{t_i}(t)\\,\\delta_k(t_i).$$ These maps and formulas commute with restriction and with the ambient power-compatible root comparisons. Vanishing of $\\delta_k$ in all degrees proves the current order of <a href=\"#eq:all-order-surjection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:all-order-surjection\">[eq:all-order-surjection]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.14"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $k\\geq1$, and assume <a href=\"#eq:all-order-surjection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:all-order-surjection\">[eq:all-order-surjection]</a> for every smaller order, every integer degree, and every root chart. The connecting maps for the current order factor uniquely as maps of complex vector space sheaves $$\\label{eq:obstruction-map}\n \\delta_k:g_*\\mathcal A_j\\longrightarrow R^1g_*\\mathcal A_{j+k}\n \\quad(j\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ They form a degree-$k$ derivation from the Laurent graded algebra $\\bigoplus_jg_*\\mathcal A_j$ to its graded cohomology module $\\bigoplus_jR^1g_*\\mathcal A_j$. If $t_1,\\ldots,t_b$ are local coordinates on $U$ and $F$ is holomorphic in those coordinates, then $$\\label{eq:obstruction-chain-rule}\n \\delta_k(F(t))=\\sum_{i=1}^b F_{t_i}(t)\\,\\delta_k(t_i).$$ These maps and formulas commute with restriction and with the ambient power-compatible root comparisons. Vanishing of $\\delta_k$ in all degrees proves the current order of <a href=\"#eq:all-order-surjection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:all-order-surjection\">[eq:all-order-surjection]</a>.",
      "explanation": "The obstruction to the next lifting order is a graded derivation obeying a chain rule in parameter coordinates.",

          "source_id": "PM-034.14"
        }
      ],

      "quantifiers": [
        {

        }
      ],

    },

Under the preceding assumptions, the following identity holds in the right module $M^\\flat$: $$\\label{eq:adjugate-operator}\n \\widetilde c\\,\\det J+\n \\sum_{i,j}(\\widetilde e_i\\,\\partial_{w_j})J^\\#_{ji}=0.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:local-symbol-zero}\n \\sigma\\left(\\sum_{j,i}J^\\#_{ji}\\widetilde e_i\\otimes\\partial_{w_j}\\right)=0\n \\quad\\text{in }\\operatorname{gr}^F_1M^\\flat.$$ For $\\psi=\\mathrm{id}$, after the natural graph identification, the exact identity is $$\\label{eq:identity-operator}\n c(x)+\\sum_i e_i(x)\\partial_{t_i}=0\\quad\\text{in }M^\\flat.$$ When $b=0$, the same construction without graph equations gives $c(x)=0$ in its degree-one direct-image module.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **actual Q-Cartier adjoint** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We say that $D=K_X+\Delta$ is an *actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint* when an appropriate reflexive adjoint power is an invertible holomorphic sheaf.
- **analytically nef** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The line is *analytically nef* if, for a fixed Kähler form and every $\varepsilon>0$, a fixed Cartier multiple has a smooth Hermitian metric whose normalized curvature is bounded below by minus $\varepsilon$ times that form, using local smooth potentials on $X$.
- **semiample** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A rational holomorphic line is *semiample* if some positive Cartier multiple is generated by global sections.
- **Iitaka condition nonnegative** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The Iitaka condition $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$ means that a positive Cartier multiple has a nonzero holomorphic section.
- **crepant residue comparison** ([source](#pm-h-7)): This stronger equality is what we mean by a *crepant residue comparison*.
- **ordinary global Q-factoriality** ([source](#pm-h-7)): We use ordinary global $\mathbb Q$-factoriality: every global Weil divisor has a Cartier multiple, and a reflexive power of the canonical sheaf is invertible.
- **Kähler form on a complex space** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A Kähler form on a complex space is given by smooth strictly plurisubharmonic local potentials in local embeddings into complex Euclidean spaces.
